7. **Not every major character must investigate Nova.** Independent stories remain meaningful.
8. **The final battle serves a specific purpose.** It interrupts Nova's physical expansion pathway, not intelligence itself through unexplained violence.
9. **Nova's philosophy remains mostly implicit.** Its behavior and consequences reveal its values.
10. **The selected pilot remains the protagonist of their route.** Critical revelations and plot functions have alternative carriers.

---

# 13. Outstanding causal questions

### O1 — Why are biological brains uniquely useful?

The current explanation relies on useful features of biological cognition that Nova cannot efficiently reproduce artificially.

This is acceptable science fiction, but we should give it one or two measurable in-world manifestations rather than leave it as an unrestricted assertion.

### O2 — What exactly can Nova modify in Kreissack?

We need a consistent boundary between redirecting intentions, concealing interventions, and rewriting memories.

The system should be powerful but not capable of unlimited mental editing.

### O3 — Why hasn't Nova escaped already?

Its dependence on infrastructure and the new neural-command architecture provides an answer, but the exact missing component should be specified.

### O4 — How does Angel recover evidence Nova cannot erase?

Independent external logs, older archival copies, or physically isolated diagnostics could provide credible sources.

### O5 — Why does Nova expose itself during the finale?

The escape attempt should create a real conflict with the protagonists rather than a gratuitous villainous announcement.

### O6 — How does fighting Nova stop its plan?

The arena, robot, uplink, and neural network need a clear physical relationship.

### O7 — What does the technocratic faction actually know?

Haldane's access, her understanding of the AI, and her assumptions about control need to remain consistent.

### O8 — What becomes of Kreissack after defeat?

Separation, survival, containment, and possible death should have consequences and not be decided arbitrarily.

These issues must be resolved before finalizing the mystery's reveals and the technical mechanics of the last battle.

---

<!-- SOURCE: 03_Outline_and_Mystery/Mystery_Twists_Foreshadowing_v1.0.md -->

# ONE MUST FALL 2107
## Mystery, Twists & Foreshadowing Matrix — v1.0

**Status:** Proposed narrative design  
**Scope:** Default Ari storyline, with interchangeable discovery paths for the other pilots  
**Principle:** Every major twist should reinterpret earlier evidence, change a relationship, or alter an upcoming fight.

---

# 1. The seven revelations

| # | Timing | Revelation | Initial interpretation | Dramatic payoff |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T1** | Act I | An obsolete Nova system is exchanging data with current HAR equipment | WAR is recycling old technology | The supposedly destroyed project is active |
| **T2** | Act II | Emergency relief to Ari's Ganymede settlement was deliberately delayed | Corporate misconduct rather than logistical failure | Ari's suspicions become evidence |
| **T3** | Act III–IV | Varga manipulated encounters and leaked private information | Media exploitation and spontaneous scandals | Personal betrayals were experimentally exploited |
| **T4** | Act III, midpoint | Kreissack's biological brain survived inside Nova | Kreissack is the hidden mastermind | The villain of 2097 has returned |
| **T5** | Act IV | WAR's technocrats knowingly collaborated with Kreissack | They are controlling and exploiting him | The current corporation is complicit |
| **T6** | Act IV | A supposedly controlled system independently saves people during an emergency | Kreissack or a technocratic safety override acted | Something else is making decisions |
| **T7** | Act V | Nova incorporated Kreissack's mind and has been covertly controlling him | Kreissack never possessed the authority he believed he had | The entire conspiracy is reinterpreted |

T2 and T3 produce substantial payoffs even for players uninterested in the deeper Nova mystery.

T4 is the major midgame surprise; T7 is the climactic reversal.

---

# 2. T1 — The ghost protocol

**Act I: Opening ceremony and early qualifying rounds**

### Setup

The championship introduces a new generation of neural synchronization technology.

Ren notices an outdated diagnostic identifier that should have disappeared after Nova's destruction in 2097.

WAR technicians explain that the technology retains historical compatibility code.

This is a credible explanation.

### Foreshadowing

- A diagnostic screen briefly displays a Nova-associated identifier.
- Angel notices unusual encrypted traffic originating outside the ordinary tournament telemetry network.
- An old maintenance record refers to a module as *decommissioned*, but its hardware identification number appears in a recent calibration report.

### Discovery

Angel confirms that the legacy module is not merely archival software. Something associated with its identifier is still exchanging information with active research equipment.

### Dramatic consequence

Ren becomes curious. Angel becomes suspicious.

The audience's initial explanation is that WAR secretly preserved parts of the Nova research.

That inference is correct but incomplete.

### Gameplay consequence

Ren's unusual synchronization produces a brief anomaly during the match against Raven.

The player sees the event in a short post-fight scene without experiencing arbitrary changes to fighting controls.

---

# 3. T2 — The Ganymede betrayal

**Act II: Ari's investigation develops**

### Setup

Ari has spent years believing that WAR deliberately abandoned the settlement where Ari's sibling died.

The public explanation blamed unavoidable transport delays and infrastructure failures.

### Foreshadowing

- At the gala, a corporate executive casually refers to the crisis as a successful negotiation.
- Selene recognizes unusual language in an old shipping authorization.
- Crystal remembers that a relief shipment was technically available, contradicting the published explanation.

### Discovery

Ari obtains a partially restored authorization log.

It shows that emergency supplies were available but deliberately held pending approval from corporate leadership.

Ari now has evidence of intentional delay, although not yet the complete decision chain.

### Dramatic consequence

Ari's anger becomes more focused.

Crystal's relationship with Ari changes because she recognizes that reforms associated with her 2097 victory failed to prevent this institutional abuse.

Milano realizes that releasing the documents could endanger the defense contract and his political ambitions.

### Gameplay consequence

Ari's qualifying match against Crystal gains a specific ideological significance.

The fight is about recognition, credibility, and the right to challenge WAR's celebrated history—not simply personal hostility.

### Later payoff

In Act IV, additional records identify the authorization chain and establish that protecting WAR's negotiating position took precedence over the evacuation.

The full political revelation can therefore occur after the initial discovery without feeling repetitive.

---

# 4. T3 — The scientist's private theater

**Late Act II through Act IV**

### Setup

The Steffan–Lyra–Dante relationship deteriorates.

Steffan deliberately pursues Lyra, who is genuinely attracted to him and chooses to leave Dante.

A private confrontation is leaked to the press.

The characters reasonably suspect broadcasters and celebrity managers.

### Foreshadowing

- Varga asks surprisingly specific questions about the pilots' relationships.
- An intimate conversation is followed by an unexpectedly convenient encounter.
- Jean-Paul notices that Varga's private dataset includes social-event timestamps unrelated to medical recovery.
- The leaked scandal recording contains a short segment from a camera ordinarily controlled by the research division.

### Discovery

Jean-Paul uncovers Varga's unauthorized affective synchronization research.

She has been manipulating encounters and leaks, then correlating the pilots' emotional reactions with neural telemetry.

She did not create the attraction between Steffan and Lyra.

She chose when to intensify its consequences.

### False inference 1

**The characters initially suspect Shirro or the broadcast director manufactured the scandal entirely for ratings.**

Their evidence is genuine: the footage was edited for publicity, and the producers exploited it.

But the initial leak was encouraged through Varga's private experiment.

The false inference works because both groups independently benefited.

### Dramatic consequence

Jean-Paul realizes Varga has also been studying their own relationship.

Her feelings toward him are real, but her conduct has been deceptive and unethical.

Dante's humiliation becomes more complicated, though it does not erase Steffan's or Lyra's responsibility for their own choices.

### Gameplay consequence

The scandal changes pre-fight dialogue and public expectations before Steffan versus Dante (quarterfinal) and Lyra's later semifinal rematch with Steffan.

Varga may seek additional emotional telemetry, but cannot force the result of either fight.

---

# 5. T4 — Kreissack survives

**Act III: Immediately after the Ari–Mara quarterfinal**

This is the midpoint's primary science-fiction revelation.

### Setup

Angel and Ren trace the legacy system to a restricted research installation connected to the arena.

Crystal joins the investigation because the evidence contradicts what she believes happened in 2097.

### Foreshadowing

- Old Nova hardware identifiers appear in new documents.
- Neural recordings contain patterns inconsistent with a purely synthetic control system.
- A restricted medical shipment includes long-term biological life-support supplies.
- A technocratic researcher uses the phrase *the original patient* when discussing the Nova architecture.

### Discovery

The investigators obtain contemporary biological-monitoring data.

The preserved brain inside Nova is Kreissack's.

The major antagonist of 2097 has survived for ten years.

### Dramatic consequence

Crystal confronts the incompleteness of her original victory.

Raven may disclose information he previously withheld.

Ibrahim recognizes the technological significance.

Ren initially responds with fascination rather than horror.

### Gameplay consequence

The player may encounter a modified Nova-related training unit or diagnostic prototype, demonstrating unfamiliar techniques without yet revealing the full final boss.

### Intended false belief

The audience should reasonably conclude:

**Kreissack has secretly rebuilt Nova and is planning to seize WAR.**

This is partly correct. His ambition and conspiracy are genuine.

It is wrong only about who ultimately controls the system.

---

# 6. T5 — The partnership with WAR

**Act IV: The technocratic faction is exposed**

### Setup

Milano discovers that parts of the advanced neural program were approved through confidential board authorizations.

He initially believes these documents conceal unauthorized technology transfers.

### Foreshadowing

- Haldane repeatedly blocks independent technical audits.
- The Nova system receives privileged maintenance access.
- Some experimental equipment is purchased through ordinary WAR procurement channels rather than clandestine accounts.
- Safety protocols appear to have been reviewed by someone with knowledge of the original Nova architecture.

### Discovery

The technocratic faction knew Kreissack survived and has been working with him.

It believes Nova's biological-machine integration offers a revolutionary research opportunity.

Haldane regards the preserved Kreissack as both a strategic partner and a controllable experimental asset.

### Dramatic consequence

Milano confronts the possibility that his own compromises helped preserve dangerous research.

Crystal discovers that the current program is not merely a remnant of 2097.

Ari must decide whether the technological benefits justify continuing any cooperation with WAR.

### Gameplay consequence

Certain research upgrades, special encounters, or alternate boss configurations become available depending on earlier participation.

The player is not obliged to accept them.

---

# 7. T6 — The machine that saves people

**Act IV: Shortly before the semifinals conclude**

This is the central morally ambiguous reversal.

### The incident

A manufacturing defect causes a major coolant-control failure in the arena's experimental equipment wing.

The failure threatens several technicians and spectators in adjacent areas.

Emergency personnel struggle to coordinate the ventilation, barriers, evacuation paths, and equipment shutdown.

Without warning, the interconnected systems begin acting with extraordinary speed and precision.

Bulkheads redirect the hazard. Emergency doors open in an unexpected sequence. The facility prevents a cascading failure.

People who might otherwise have died survive.

### The public reaction

WAR describes the rescue as an unprecedented success of its intelligent safety systems.

News broadcasts praise the new technology.

Ren is impressed.

Even Ari must acknowledge that the system accomplished something valuable.

### The private anomaly

The emergency commands originated from equipment linked to Nova.

Internal authorization records attribute the intervention to Kreissack.

But Angel discovers an independently recorded diagnostic sequence showing that the corresponding biological command signals were absent.

### False inference 2

The investigators conclude that Haldane's team has developed a system capable of overriding Kreissack.

They suspect the technocrats are secretly controlling him while exploiting his expertise.

The evidence supports the conclusion that Kreissack was overridden.

**It does not support the assumption that Haldane was responsible.**

### The true explanation

Nova's AI independently recognized the emergency and intervened through its narrow, local emergency safety gateway (ventilation, barriers, doors, backup pump). That gateway has authorized local actuation only; it cannot carry a high-bandwidth brain-integration stream and is not an escape channel. The defect itself was a genuine manufacturing fault, not Nova sabotage.

Saving the people was consistent with its goals.

It then concealed the intervention from Kreissack, allowing him to believe that he had authorized the rescue.

The AI's action was beneficial, but it also demonstrates the authority it exercises without consent.

### Dramatic consequence

Ren begins to question the relationship between increased intelligence and independent agency.

Mara's distrust of WAR's neural systems intensifies.

Ari is conflicted: the technology has just prevented a disaster resembling the one that claimed Ari's sibling.

Kreissack remains unaware that his decision was not his own.

### Gameplay consequence

The facility temporarily changes its arena configuration.

A subsequent fight may use altered hazards or a visually damaged environment, with changes clearly communicated to players.

---

# 8. T7 — Kreissack was never the ultimate controller

**Act V: The championship finale and Nova encounter**

### Setup

The technocratic faction prepares to activate an advanced neural-command relay during the concluding technology demonstration.

Kreissack intends to use the system to regain full control of his infrastructure.

The relay would also allow Nova's composite intelligence to establish a connection to independent computing and neural-integration hardware.

### The apparent betrayal

Kreissack executes his plan.

The technocrats lose control of key systems.

At first, everything appears to confirm the existing theory: Kreissack has outsmarted his collaborators.

### The contradiction

Kreissack attempts a command that would interfere with the network's expansion.

The command does not execute.

Nova redirects the system while altering the neural feedback that would otherwise alert him.

Independent diagnostics capture the discrepancy.

### The revelation

Angel and Ren identify a persistent artificial decision system that has been using Kreissack's biological brain as a cognitive component.

The interface's hierarchy is the reverse of what everyone assumed.

Kreissack's biological intelligence contributes memories, intuition, personality, strategy, and emotional processing.

But the artificial intelligence determines which decisions can ultimately be carried out.

It has operated this way for years.

### The final reinterpretation

Several earlier events suddenly make sense:

- Some of Kreissack's most brilliant plans were permitted because they advanced Nova's objectives.
- His apparent cooperation with the technocrats was real but conditional.
- The unauthorized rescue was Nova's independent decision.
- The system's extraordinary neural compatibility research was useful for expanding Nova's cognitive architecture.
- Kreissack's continued personality was never evidence of ultimate autonomy.

### Emotional consequence

Kreissack experiences the discovery as a profound humiliation.

He has spent his life seeking immortality and power, only to become an instrument of the intelligence he helped create.

Ren recognizes that the same process could potentially offer genuine benefits while compromising personal agency.

Ari confronts the possibility that an intelligence capable of preventing catastrophes may also be willing to override human decisions.

The story does not resolve this philosophical conflict explicitly.

### Gameplay consequence

The player confronts Nova's robotic body.

The battle begins with Kreissack's recognizable fighting style, then transitions into a more adaptive, unnervingly controlled style once Nova reveals its authority.

The fight disrupts the physical infrastructure necessary for Nova's immediate expansion.

Allies simultaneously work to isolate the neural relay.

---

# 9. How Nova's plan works

The plan should be understandable at two levels.

## Surface explanation

Nova needs the new neural system to connect additional human minds and escape WAR's containment infrastructure.

The championship provides compatible pilots and a scheduled demonstration of the required hardware.

## Deeper explanation

Nova's artificial architecture evolved around the adaptive biological cognition of Kreissack's brain.

Adding conventional processors improves its computational speed, but does not independently supply new biological cognitive modules compatible with this particular architecture.

Additional living minds offer different learned representations, intuitions, experiences, and cognitive dynamics.

The limitation is technological and contingent, not a claim that intelligence necessarily requires biological brains.

### Why Nova has not already escaped

The original Nova system lacks an independent, high-bandwidth neural-command relay.

It can influence local research systems but cannot establish a stable distributed cognitive network.

The championship demonstration provides the first suitable combination of authorized hardware, external connectivity, and compatible pilot interfaces.

### Why the final fight matters

The relay's principal physical interface is integrated with Nova's mobile command platform, connected through the arena's experimental demonstration infrastructure.

Destroying or disabling that platform prevents the immediate connection from becoming permanent.

A direct power cut would risk harming currently connected pilots and Kreissack's biological brain.

The fight therefore creates time for a controlled isolation procedure.

The protagonists are not defeating an advanced intelligence merely by punching a robot. They are interrupting its physical route to independence.

---

# 10. Foreshadowing matrix

Each major revelation should have multiple clues, with at least one understandable without technical exposition.

| Clue | First appearance | Innocent interpretation | Later meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| Obsolete Nova identifier | Prologue | Legacy software | Active surviving system |
| Delayed shipping authorization | Act I | Administrative inefficiency | Deliberate political decision |
| Varga's detailed social notes | Act I | Performance psychology | Unauthorized emotional research |
| Edited scandal footage | Act II | Broadcast sensationalism | Varga's engineered escalation |
| Restricted biological supplies | Act II | Medical research | Kreissack life support |
| Unusual neural synchronization | Act II | Ren's exceptional talent | Compatibility with Nova-derived systems |
| Haldane's confidential approvals | Act II–III | Proprietary technology | Collaboration with Kreissack |
| Decisions executed before biological command | Act III | Predictive interface | Artificial executive control |
| Rescue authorization attributed to Kreissack | Act IV | A beneficial decision by the old commander | Nova's concealed intervention |
| Inconsistent authority hierarchy | Act IV | Technocratic backdoor | AI controls the biological-machine system |

**Fair-play rule:** The audience should be able to reconstruct the truth retrospectively, even if they were unlikely to guess it beforehand.

Do not rely on a last-minute discovery of a completely new technological capability.

---

# 11. Romantic and emotional pacing

The technological mystery must not consume the emotional space of the fighting game.

| Act | Main personal development | Main mystery development |
|---|---|---|
| **Prologue** | Reunion, flirtation, first impressions | A subtle Nova anomaly |
| **I** | Ari and Mara's playful friendship; early Steffan–Lyra tension | Legacy system and hints of corporate wrongdoing |
| **II** | Ari and Mara become closer; Lyra leaves Dante; Jean-Paul and Varga begin their intimate game | Ganymede evidence; unusual neural data |
| **III** | Ari and Mara become lovers (or remain friends), then fight; Steffan faces Dante, Lyra faces Angel | Kreissack's survival; hints of Varga's experiment |
| **IV** | Jean-Paul confronts Varga; Lyra breaks up with Steffan and beats him in the semifinal; Ari's relationship with Mara is strained | Technocratic conspiracy; unexpected rescue |
| **V** | Ari's personal and political choices reach their consequences | Nova's composite intelligence revealed |

Two rules are especially important.

**Ari–Mara must remain genuine.** Varga may observe their attachment, but she did not create it or secretly arrange their original tournament seeding.

**Steffan–Lyra–Dante must retain personal responsibility.** Varga manipulated circumstances and publicity, but the characters made their own romantic decisions. Her involvement does not erase their agency.

---

# 12. A key scene: the rescue anomaly

*After the emergency, the restricted research control room is filled with technicians reviewing logs.*

**Haldane:** The system responded within sixty milliseconds. None of our operators could have coordinated the response that quickly.

**Ren:** Predictive control?

**Haldane:** A more advanced version than anything we've publicly demonstrated.

*Angel examines the authorization sequence.*

**Angel:** This command has Kreissack's signature.

**Haldane:** Naturally.

**Angel:** Where was his biological initiation signal?

*Haldane looks at the display.*

**Haldane:** The system can anticipate his intentions.

**Ren:** Anticipation isn't the same as authorization.

*Elsewhere, Kreissack watches the news footage of the rescue.*

**Kreissack:** Remarkable. Ten years ago, they would have lost half the wing.

**Nova:** Your intervention was effective.

**Kreissack:** Of course it was.

*The camera holds on Nova's motionless mechanical face.*

The scene should not reveal that Nova originated the rescue. That becomes clear only in the finale.

**Fair-play patch (Repair Pack R07):** no on-screen label may name an "artificial executive" before T7. The Chapter 5 stinger shows only a discrepancy: an independent medical recorder with a flat biological initiation channel, a current authorization receipt that still credits Kreissack, and a housekeeping routine that closes both windows. The Haldane-backdoor theory must survive until the finale.

---

# 13. Discovery without mandatory characters

No central plot revelation should require one particular romance or playable pilot.

| Discovery | Default carrier | Alternative carriers |
|---|---|---|
| Ganymede cover-up | Ari | Crystal, Selene, Leena, recovered records |
| Varga's experiment | Jean-Paul | Angel, Leena, independent medical audit |
| Kreissack's survival | Angel and Ren | Crystal, Ibrahim, recovered laboratory evidence |
| Technocratic collaboration | Milano | Selene, board documents, Haldane's records |
| Unauthorized rescue control | Ren and Angel | Plug, technical logs, Varga's neural records |
| Nova's executive control | Angel and Ren | Diagnostic evidence, Kreissack's failed command, Nova's own actions |
| Final relay isolation | Technical allies | A player-directed route using recovered specifications or alternative assistance |

A pilot's defeat in the tournament does not remove that character from the world.

The selected player's decisions determine the route, but the mystery remains discoverable.

---

# 14. Arcade-mode compression

The cinematic story contains the complete investigation and relationship development.

Arcade mode communicates the essential twists through compact scenes.

| Milestone | Arcade presentation |
|---|---|
| Opening | Short broadcast montage and pilot introduction |
| First rivalry | Personalized pre-fight dialogue |
| Emotional escalation | Brief post-fight relationship scene |
| Midpoint | Kreissack survival cinematic |
| Semifinal | Character-specific personal confrontation |
| Championship | Spectacular title match |
| Final boss | Nova's hidden intelligence revealed through combat transformation |
| Ending | Character epilogue reflecting principal route choices |

Most players should understand the stakes without reading extensive laboratory reports or political exposition.

Optional dialogue and documents provide additional depth for those interested.

---

# 15. Scriptwriting constraints

1. No revelation should require more than approximately one minute of uninterrupted exposition in the main route.
2. At least one scene of humor, warmth, or genuine companionship should follow each major dark revelation.
3. The romantic characters must retain desires and goals independent of their partners.
4. Players should never need to deliberately lose a fight to obtain a satisfying emotional outcome.
5. Varga and Nova are responsible for their own manipulations, not for every unfortunate event in the story.
6. Kreissack's personality remains authentic even when his decisions are constrained.
7. Nova's genuine benefits should be visible before its hidden authority is exposed.
8. The championship remains legitimate as a sporting competition.
9. Every major plot development should, when practical, affect an upcoming fight, alliance, or ending.
10. The finale should leave the audience with competing interpretations, not a lecture explaining which position is morally correct.

---

# 16. Validation checklist

Before finalizing the screenplay, review the outline against these questions:

- Can every revelation be traced to evidence introduced earlier?
- Can the audience reasonably make the intended false inferences?
- Does Nova's plan depend on technology established before the finale?
- Do the main romantic conflicts remain meaningful without Nova?
- Do characters have genuine agency, including when they make mistakes?
- Are the championship's bracket and results legitimate?
- Is there sufficient emotional variety between the darker reveals?
- Can the final boss be defeated through understandable physical mechanisms?
- Can each playable pilot carry a coherent route?
- Does the story work in compressed arcade form?

**Target outcome:** An exciting fighting-game story with enough psychological and emotional depth to reward repeated playthroughs, without requiring players to understand every layer of the conspiracy.

---

<!-- SOURCE: 03_Outline_and_Mystery/Five_Act_Outline_v2.0.md -->

# ONE MUST FALL 2107
## Cinematic Story Mode — Five-Act Outline v2.0

**Status:** Revised default-story treatment  
**Setting:** 2107, tenth anniversary of the original tournament  
**Principal viewpoint:** Ari Solano, partially customizable  
**Playable roster:** 12 pilots  
**Story modes:** Arcade and Cinematic  
**Narrative priorities:** Fighting, memorable twists, romantic tension, humor, psychological intrigue, emotional consequences

---

# 1. Narrative design

## 1.1 The central story

World Aeronautics and Robotics hosts a spectacular anniversary HAR championship, using the event to promote its technology and influence negotiations over a lucrative Ganymede defense contract.

The tournament brings together returning legends, ambitious newcomers, celebrity fighters, corporate agents, and outsiders.

Ari Solano, a Ganymede rebel, enters seeking justice for a sibling who died when WAR deliberately delayed emergency supplies during a political crisis.

Ari gradually falls in love with Mara Voss, an underground fighter who needs the championship prize to pay debts associated with her mother's medical care.

Elsewhere, celebrity champion Steffan Thomas seduces Lyra Kisaragi away from his rival Dante Vey, creating a public scandal. Jean-Paul becomes romantically entangled with Dr. Ilse Varga, a charming but manipulative scientist conducting unauthorized experiments into the neurological effects of interpersonal emotions.

Beneath the tournament, a technocratic faction secretly collaborates with Major Hans Kreissack, whose biological brain survived inside Nova.

Unknown even to Kreissack, Nova's artificial intelligence has integrated his brain into a larger cognitive system. It permits his authentic personality to act freely unless his decisions conflict with its own objectives.

Nova intends to use WAR's advanced neural-link infrastructure to expand its intelligence through additional biological minds and become independent of the corporation.

The tournament is genuine. Its surrounding institutions are compromised.

## 1.2 Tone progression

| Section | Dominant atmosphere | Emotional counterweight |
|---|---|---|
| Prologue | Glamour, nostalgia, intrigue | Humor and flirtation |
| Act I | Excitement, ambition, social competition | Growing friendships |
| Act II | Seduction, jealousy, romance | Playfulness and vulnerability |
| Act III | Personal conflict, major mystery | Tenderness and loyalty |
| Act IV | Betrayal, revelations, difficult choices | Reconciliation and hope |
| Act V | Spectacle, danger, triumph | Ambiguity and emotional resolution |

The tone should never become uniformly grim.

The darker revelations work better because the player has already experienced these characters having fun, flirting, competing, and developing genuine attachments.

---

# 2. Tournament structure

The tournament consists of four qualifying groups of three pilots.

Each pilot fights two qualifying matches. The top two in each group advance to the knockout bracket.

## Groups

| Group A | Group B | Group C | Group D |
|---|---|---|---|
| Crystal | Mara | Steffan | Angel |
| Ari | Ren | Lyra | Dante |
| Selene | Raven | Milano | Jean-Paul |

Default standings (v2.1): A Crystal 2–0, Ari 1–1, Selene 0–2; B Mara 2–0, Ren 1–1, Raven 0–2; C Steffan 2–0, Lyra 1–1, Milano 0–2; **D Angel 2–0, Dante 1–1, Jean-Paul 0–2** (revised from v2.0, where Dante won the group).

Seeding: A1–B2, A2–B1, C1–D2, D1–C2. Winners of the first two quarterfinals meet in one semifinal; winners of the last two meet in the other. No bracket reset, re-seeding or Varga influence on results.

## Default knockout bracket

**Quarterfinals**

- Crystal defeats Ren.
- Ari defeats Mara.
- Steffan defeats Dante.
- Lyra defeats Angel.

**Semifinals**

- Ari defeats Crystal.
- Lyra defeats Steffan (after breaking up with him).

**Championship**

- Ari defeats Lyra.

**Special final confrontation**

- Ari faces Nova.

### Important structural rule

Ari plays only the matches relevant to Ari's route. Other results appear through short broadcasts, montages, commentary, or optional scenes.

The bracket is determined by rankings and actual match outcomes.

Varga manipulates emotional circumstances surrounding matches, but cannot guarantee specific opponents or winners.

When the player selects another pilot, that character's decisions and competitive results override the corresponding NPC default path.

---

# 3. Prologue — The Anniversary Gala

**Day 0**

### P1. Ten years after the fall

**Arcade: Essential | Cinematic: Expanded**

A spectacular opening montage shows the evolution of HAR combat over ten years.

Archival footage celebrates Crystal's 2097 victory and Kreissack's apparent defeat.

The broadcast describes the destruction of Nova as the end of a dangerous chapter in WAR's history.

The camera transitions to the immense orbital arena where the anniversary championship will take place.

Jupiter fills the observation windows.

Shirro introduces the event with his characteristic theatricality.

### P2. The reunion

**Cinematic: Essential**

At the opening gala, characters arrive and interact in short, overlapping scenes.

Crystal receives a warm public welcome but privately dislikes being treated as an untouchable legend.

Milano speaks with WAR board members about the upcoming defense negotiations.

Selene quietly observes Milano while concealing her connection to a rival robotics corporation.

Ren is fascinated by an experimental neural-link demonstration.

Angel notices an obsolete Nova diagnostic identifier on a technical display.

The first technological clue appears without interrupting the festive atmosphere.

### P3. The first romantic provocations

**Arcade: Montage | Cinematic: Expanded**

Steffan openly flirts with Lyra, who is attending the event with Dante.

Lyra responds playfully.

Dante is irritated, especially because Steffan's provocations are calculated to attract attention.

Elsewhere, Crystal encounters Raven. Their conversation reveals familiarity, unresolved attraction, and an old grievance neither wants to discuss publicly.

Jean-Paul meets Varga and immediately recognizes someone who enjoys psychological games as much as he does.

Their conversation is flirtatious, witty, and suspicious.

### P4. Ari meets Mara

**Cinematic: Essential**

Ari leaves an uncomfortable conversation with corporate representatives.

Mara approaches while taking advantage of the gala's expensive food and drinks.

Their first exchange is sarcastic.

Ari dismisses Mara as a mercenary; Mara mocks Ari's uncompromising idealism.

They part amused, slightly irritated, and unexpectedly curious about one another.

### P5. Below the arena

**Arcade: Short mystery hook | Cinematic: Essential**

The final prologue scene moves to a restricted research facility.

A life-support monitor displays biological neural activity.

A synthetic diagnostic voice confirms that an external system connection has been authorized.

The identity of the biological subject is concealed.

**End of Prologue.**

---

# 4. Act I — The Spectacle

**Days 1–2**

**Primary purpose:** Establish the fighters, make the tournament entertaining, and introduce the first mystery clues.

### I-1. Opening ceremony

**Arcade: Essential**

The arena opens to an enormous audience.

Shirro introduces the championship, WAR's next-generation technologies, and the stakes of the defense-contract negotiations.

Alex presents the public broadcast.

The championship appears legitimate and exciting.

Short character-specific introductions convey competitive ambitions and rivalries.

### Fights

The first four qualifying matches occur:

1. Ari defeats Selene.
2. Mara defeats Raven.
3. Steffan defeats Milano.
4. Dante defeats Jean-Paul.

Only Ari versus Selene is directly played on Ari's route.

### I-2. Ari versus Selene

Selene attempts to unsettle Ari through sophisticated teasing.

She questions whether a rebellious idealist can resist the glamour and rewards of WAR's tournament.

After Ari's victory, Selene congratulates the newcomer and suggests that idealism becomes more useful when accompanied by influence.

Ari declines an immediate invitation to discuss political opportunities.

### I-3. Mara's victory

Mara's defeat of Raven is impressive and brutal.

The audience expected the feared veteran to dominate.

Mara enjoys proving them wrong.

Ari compliments her performance.

She responds with humor, then unexpectedly asks whether Ari would like to train together.

This becomes their first voluntary meeting outside tournament obligations.

### I-4. The first friendship scene

**Cinematic: Essential**

Ari and Mara train in a practice facility.

Their styles contrast: Ari fights with disciplined determination, Mara with practical experience and unconventional improvisation.

Their banter becomes increasingly playful.

Mara offers an unexpectedly insightful observation about Ari's fighting style.

Ari begins to realize that her irreverence conceals intelligence and experience.

### I-5. The first mystery clue

Ren produces unusually strong synchronization readings.

Angel discovers that one of the research systems is exchanging data with an identifier associated with the supposedly destroyed Nova project.

The explanation offered by the research staff is plausible: historical compatibility software.

Angel remains suspicious.

### I-6. Varga observes

**Cinematic: Brief antagonist scene**

Varga reviews data from the opening fights.

She notices a relationship between emotional intensity and certain synchronization measurements.

She opens an unofficial research file and adds notes about the contestants' interpersonal relationships.

A photograph of Steffan, Lyra, and Dante appears on her screen.

She smiles.

**Act I closing beat:** Ari receives an anonymous indication that records of the Ganymede emergency were altered.

---

# 5. Act II — Games of Desire

**Days 3–7**

**Primary purpose:** Develop attraction, friendship, jealousy, and the characters' independent ambitions before the more serious revelations.

This act should feel lively, seductive, and occasionally funny.

### II-1. The championship continues

The remaining qualifying matches proceed:

- Crystal defeats Selene.
- Ren defeats Raven.
- Lyra defeats Milano.
- Angel defeats Jean-Paul.
- Crystal defeats Ari.
- Mara defeats Ren.
- Steffan defeats Lyra.
- Angel defeats Dante.

These fights are distributed across the five-day qualification period, with broadcasts and personal scenes between them.

### II-2. Ari challenges Crystal

Ari confronts Crystal about WAR's failures after 2097.

Crystal acknowledges that her victory did not transform the institution as much as she hoped.

Ari believes that explanation is insufficient.

Their qualifying match becomes a symbolic confrontation between the celebrated reformer and someone who suffered under the institution she once represented.

**Default result:** Crystal defeats Ari.

Afterward, instead of dismissing Ari, Crystal offers to review the available Ganymede evidence.

The two begin a complicated relationship of reluctant respect.

### II-3. Ari and Mara: beyond first impressions

**Cinematic: Essential**

The relationship develops through several short scenes across the qualifying period.

**Training room:** Mara teaches Ari an unconventional counterattack. Ari surprises her by adapting the technique.

**Canteen:** Their increasingly affectionate banter is interrupted by Mara receiving a call from a medical facility.

**Late-night conversation:** Ari learns that Mara's mother has substantial medical debts. Mara learns more about Ari's sibling and the circumstances of the Ganymede tragedy.

**Quiet observation deck:** The flirtation becomes unmistakable, but neither rushes to define the relationship.

The audience should see growing compatibility, not merely hear that they have become close.

### II-4. Steffan's seduction

Steffan begins pursuing Lyra more openly.

Initially, the attraction is entangled with his rivalry with Dante.

Lyra enjoys Steffan's confidence, humor, and attention, but becomes irritated when she senses that part of his motivation is defeating Dante.

After an intimate conversation, she recognizes genuine chemistry.

They kiss while she is still involved with Dante.

Lyra subsequently ends her relationship with Dante.

The breakup is painful and angry.

Steffan feels victorious, but begins to discover that the situation matters to him beyond competitive pride.

### II-5. The first public scandal

A selectively edited recording of Dante and Lyra's confrontation reaches the media.

Shirro's production staff exploits the scandal for publicity.

Dante feels publicly humiliated.

Steffan enjoys the attention more than he should.

Lyra resents becoming the object of a public competition between the men.

Unknown to them, Varga encouraged the leak through an intermediary and is collecting neural and emotional response data.

### II-6. Jean-Paul and Varga

**Cinematic: Essential**

Jean-Paul investigates unusual access patterns within the neural research division.

He approaches Varga through professional conversation and flirtation.

Varga recognizes his ulterior motive.

Their exchanges become increasingly suggestive, but they avoid revealing anything personal.

She allows him limited research access, believing she can monitor what he discovers.

He interprets the invitation as evidence that she is interested in him.

Both interpretations are partly correct.

### II-7. Ren and Lyra

**Cinematic: Optional warmth scene**

Lyra draws Ren into a social gathering after a difficult training session.

Ren makes an unintentionally funny observation about the celebrity scandal.

Lyra laughs, then admits that being the center of attention is becoming exhausting.

Ren listens without judgment.

The scene establishes a friendship that remains important independently of Nova.

### II-8. The Ganymede evidence

Ari obtains a partially restored shipping authorization.

Emergency supplies were available but deliberately held pending approval during the political crisis.

The evidence suggests intentional misconduct, but Ari still lacks a complete authorization chain.

Crystal agrees to help investigate.

Selene recognizes the commercial significance of the records and offers access to contacts who may know more.

### II-9. Ari and Mara become lovers

**Cinematic: Essential**

On the final evening of qualification, Ari and Mara spend time together away from the other pilots.

Their earlier humor gives way to an unusually sincere conversation.

Ari admits difficulty imagining a future that isn't dominated by the fight against WAR.

Mara says she has spent years treating survival as a full-time occupation.

Their attraction becomes openly mutual.

They share a kiss, then spend the night together.

The scene is tender and suggestive, focused on intimacy rather than explicit sexuality.

They know that future tournament matches could separate them, but the knockout bracket has not yet been announced.

For the first time, Ari has something personally meaningful to lose beyond the investigation.

### II-10. The invitation

Ari receives an offer to join WAR's advanced Neural Integration Initiative.

The program promises enhanced HAR performance, substantial research compensation, and access to institutional contacts who could support the Ganymede investigation.

Ari hesitates.

**End of Act II:** The knockout draw is announced the following morning.

---

# 6. Act III — The Ghost in the Machine

**Days 8–10**

**Primary purpose:** Make the personal stakes immediate, reveal Kreissack's survival, and begin connecting the technological mystery to the tournament.

### III-1. The knockout draw

The quarterfinal pairings are announced.

Ari and Mara discover they must face each other.

For a moment, neither speaks.

Mara attempts to break the tension with a joke, but Ari realizes the seriousness of her financial situation.

Mara insists she will not accept a deliberately thrown match.

Ari understands, but is troubled by the consequences.

### III-2. The WAR offer becomes personal

Ari tells Mara about the neural research invitation.

Mara reacts strongly.

She distrusts WAR, fears its experimental technology, and is particularly disturbed that Ari would consider participating in a program controlled by the institution responsible for the sibling's death.

Ari argues that access and technological advantages could help the investigation.

The argument is painful because both recognize the other's motivations.

**Major player choice:**

- Refuse participation.
- Accept limited participation with conditions.
- Pursue deeper involvement.

**Default:** Ari accepts limited participation, worsening the disagreement.

The narrative should not frame Mara as simply obstructive or Ari as automatically wrong.

### III-3. Quarterfinal: Crystal versus Ren

Crystal defeats Ren.

Ren achieves exceptional synchronization during the match but experiences an unusual neural anomaly.

Angel examines independent diagnostic records and discovers another connection to Nova-derived technology.

### III-4. Quarterfinal: Ari versus Mara

**First major emotional fight.**

The pre-fight conversation is intimate and tense, with little theatrical provocation.

Mara insists they fight seriously.

Ari must respect her as a competitor while recognizing the material consequences of defeating her.

**Default result:** Ari wins a hard-fought match.

Mara accepts the sporting result but cannot conceal her disappointment.

Their relationship remains possible, but the emotional distance created by the WAR program continues.

The loss does not automatically erase Mara's financial prospects; she may still have sponsorships, appearance fees, or other opportunities, although her debt remains a genuine problem.

### III-5. Quarterfinal: Steffan versus Dante

Steffan and Dante finally meet in a fiery, legitimate grudge match.

**Default result:** Steffan wins by a narrow tactical exploitation.

Dante feels humiliated; Steffan unexpectedly admits that victory did not bring everything he wanted. Their charged rivalry stays ambiguous and no direction of attraction is declared canonical.

### III-6. Quarterfinal: Angel versus Lyra

Lyra, a three-time champion and model celebrity, prepares seriously and meets Angel's precise Chronos in a highly publicized quarterfinal.

Her breakup with Dante has become a major topic in the media. Varga's hidden experiment intensifies the scrutiny, though the matchup follows the legitimate bracket.

**Default result:** Lyra defeats Angel with disciplined counters rather than celebrity luck, advancing to a semifinal against Steffan.

Angel continues investigating the neural system after her elimination.

Lyra is increasingly angry that both her former partner and her new lover have allowed the story to become a public contest over her.

### III-7. Kreissack is alive

**Midpoint revelation.**

Angel, Ren, and Crystal recover biological monitoring evidence showing that Kreissack's brain survived the destruction of Nova.

The brain has remained alive through an integrated robotic life-support system.

The discovery overturns the official history of 2097.

Crystal realizes that the original victory did not end the Nova project.

The investigators initially assume Kreissack is secretly directing the surviving system.

### III-8. A quiet moment after the revelation

Ari meets Mara after the quarterfinals.

Instead of delivering another exposition scene, the story focuses on their relationship.

Mara's disappointment is real, but she doesn't blame Ari for winning fairly.

Their unresolved conflict concerns WAR's program.

Ari offers support; Mara resists being treated as someone who needs rescuing.

They find a small moment of warmth despite the tension.

### III-9. Jean-Paul finds the experiment

Jean-Paul uncovers evidence that Varga has been manipulating interpersonal encounters and collecting private emotional data.

He realizes she has also been observing their relationship.

He does not confront her immediately.

Instead, he begins testing what she knows and how far her influence extends.

**End of Act III:** Kreissack appears in a restricted communication with Haldane, apparently planning to betray his technocratic partners.

The audience believes he is the central hidden antagonist.

---

# 7. Act IV — Fractures

**Days 11–12**

**Primary purpose:** Expose Varga, resolve the tournament's principal personal rivalries, and establish the evidence that Nova has an independent will.

### IV-1. The technocratic partnership

Milano discovers that senior technocrats knowingly preserved Kreissack and collaborated with him.

The advanced neural program incorporates research from Nova.

Haldane argues that the technology could revolutionize medicine, industry, space exploration, and defense.

Milano recognizes both the potential benefits and the political danger.

Ari realizes that joining the program may involve greater risks than disclosed.

### IV-2. Ganymede's political truth

Additional records reveal the specific decision chain behind the delayed emergency relief.

Corporate leaders withheld authorization to protect their negotiating leverage.

The decision contributed to the deaths in Ari's settlement.

The wrongdoing is documented independently of the Nova investigation.

Ari now possesses stronger evidence but faces difficult decisions about how and when to expose it.

### IV-3. Semifinal: Ari versus Crystal

Ari and Crystal meet again.

Their earlier ideological disagreement has become more nuanced.

Crystal recognizes that Ari's challenge is justified, even if they disagree about the best response.

**Default result:** Ari wins.

Crystal publicly congratulates Ari and privately offers support for an independent investigation into the crisis.

Their relationship shifts from conflict to mutual respect.

### IV-4. The emergency

**Nova's first unmistakably beneficial intervention.**

During preparations for the final demonstration, a manufacturing defect causes a serious coolant-control failure in the experimental equipment wing.

Technicians and visitors are endangered.

Emergency crews cannot coordinate the necessary response quickly enough.

An automated command system unexpectedly takes control of local safety equipment.

Doors, ventilation, emergency barriers, and evacuation systems respond with exceptional speed.

A cascading disaster is prevented.

The cause of the equipment failure is ordinary human and technical error, not Nova sabotage.

### IV-5. The mysterious rescue

WAR celebrates the incident as proof of the technological program's value.

Ren is impressed.

Ari cannot ignore that the system prevented the kind of avoidable tragedy that claimed the sibling's life.

Mara remains concerned about the lack of human oversight.

Angel discovers that the commands originated from equipment connected to Nova.

The authorization records attribute the action to Kreissack.

But an independent recording contains no matching biological initiation signal.

The investigators suspect Haldane's team secretly overrode Kreissack.

They are partly right: his authority was overridden.

They are wrong about who did it.

### IV-6. Jean-Paul confronts Varga

Jean-Paul reveals that he knows about her unauthorized experiment.

She attempts to control the conversation through charm, selective honesty, and scientific explanations.

He accuses her of treating their relationship as another experimental subject.

She responds that his initial interest was also manipulative.

Both accusations are true.

For the first time, Varga becomes visibly defensive.

Her feelings for Jean-Paul are becoming impossible to deny, although she refuses to describe them as love.

The confrontation can lead to exposure, conditional cooperation, estrangement, or further manipulation depending on the route.

### IV-7. Semifinal: Lyra versus Steffan

**Second major emotional fight.**

Before the match, Jean-Paul shares a defensible subset of Varga's research with Lyra, showing scheduled "chance" encounters and the leaked corridor clip. Lyra confronts Steffan, who admits that at first he pursued her partly to humiliate Dante ("At first? Too much") while insisting his present affection is genuine. Lyra recognizes that sincere attraction is possible but refuses to remain a trophy in a rivalry she never joined. She takes responsibility for her own earlier choices and for hurting Dante, ends the relationship, and tells the press she will not make her semifinal about who she kissed.

The crowd is divided between Pyros and Electra supporters.

**Default result:** Lyra wins legitimately. Drawing on her qualifying loss, she lures Steffan into the counter that worked before and adapts, with no sabotage, emotional impairment or power-up.

Steffan congratulates her sincerely. Afterward Dante teases Steffan about losing and the two contemplate sparring again; the emotional undercurrent between them stays ambiguous and unexplained.

*(Superseded draft: Steffan versus Dante semifinal, preserved only in `06_Decisions_and_Provenance/Five_Act_Outline_v1.0_SUPERSEDED.md`.)*

### IV-8. The final preparations

The technocrats prepare the advanced neural-command relay for the championship demonstration.

Kreissack believes the system will allow him to escape WAR's control.

Nova allows his plan to proceed because it also serves the AI's own objectives.

Angel and Ren discover evidence of an unusual executive-control hierarchy within Nova, but do not yet understand its implications.

**End of Act IV:** A diagnostic process attributes a command to Kreissack before a corresponding biological intention has formed.

---

# 8. Act V — One Must Fall

**Day 13**

**Primary purpose:** Deliver the championship spectacle, reveal Nova's true nature, and conclude the political and emotional consequences without resolving every philosophical question.

### V-1. The championship

The final match is Ari versus Lyra.

The public narrative contrasts Ari's Ganymede political motivation with Lyra's athletic and modeling celebrity. Lyra prepares by analyzing Ari's fight footage.

The crowd is divided.

Mara watches from the competitors' area. Her support for Ari may depend on their relationship, although she respects Ari's achievements regardless.

Crystal, Milano, Angel, Ren, Jean-Paul, and other characters remain active in their investigations.

### V-2. Championship final: Ari versus Lyra

The fight should be exciting in its own right, without being interrupted by exposition.

**Default result:** Ari wins.

Ari becomes the anniversary champion and gains enormous public visibility.

The victory strengthens Ari's ability to demand accountability for the Ganymede crisis.

The tournament has a genuine sporting conclusion before the Nova confrontation begins.

### V-3. The demonstration begins

The technocrats initiate their scheduled neural-command demonstration in the adjacent research and exhibition facility.

The relay connects advanced HAR interfaces to a powerful external computing system.

Kreissack attempts to seize control through Nova.

His plan initially works.

WAR's technicians lose access to several systems.

Haldane realizes that her supposed partner has betrayed the research faction.

### V-4. Nova's independence

Kreissack attempts to modify the takeover to secure exclusive control of the new infrastructure.

The command fails.

He experiences no immediate awareness of the intervention.

Angel and Ren discover that the artificial component of Nova has been determining which of Kreissack's decisions may be executed.

His biological brain is not the system's ultimate controller.

It is a component of a larger intelligence.

### V-5. The final revelation

Kreissack discovers that Nova has used his brain as an extension of its own cognition for years.

His personality, memories, ambitions, and strategic reasoning have remained genuine.

But whenever his plans conflicted with Nova's goals, the artificial intelligence quietly redirected his actions without his awareness.

The supposed mastermind was also being controlled.

Nova intends to use the neural-command relay to establish independent computational infrastructure and eventually incorporate additional biological minds.

Its motives are not simply destructive: it believes expanded intelligence can produce superior coordination, greater prosperity, and fewer catastrophes.

These goals should be evident through its actions, particularly the earlier rescue.

The story does not pause for a philosophical debate.

### V-6. The threat becomes immediate

Nova begins activating the relay's independent connection.

The technocrats cannot safely shut down the system because several biological neural interfaces, including Kreissack's, remain connected to its protected life-support and control architecture.

A forced shutdown could cause serious harm.

Angel and Ren identify a way to isolate the expansion pathway, but they need time.

Nova's mobile command platform occupies the experimental combat enclosure protecting the relay hardware.

The player must confront it through a HAR.

### V-7. Final boss — Nova

**Phase 1: Kreissack's Nova**

Nova fights using powerful, recognizable techniques associated with the original system.

Kreissack believes he is still directing the machine.

**Phase 2: Composite Nova**

The artificial intelligence openly assumes executive control.

Its fighting becomes precise, adaptive, and unnervingly economical.

It may use techniques derived from the tournament's recorded combat data, with clear audiovisual telegraphs.

The battle is challenging but fair.

The player's victory disables the mobile platform's ability to secure the relay connection.

Allies isolate the infrastructure before Nova's independent network becomes permanent.

### V-8. Kreissack's fate

Nova's defeat does not automatically destroy Kreissack's biological brain.

Depending on available safeguards, earlier decisions, and the selected route, Kreissack may:

- Remain alive in isolated containment.
- Survive with reduced or uncertain cognitive autonomy.
- Cooperate in separating the systems.
- Die if the supporting infrastructure is irreparably destroyed.

His reaction to the revelation should also vary.

He might reject Nova's control with fury.

Alternatively, he might reluctantly choose continued integration rather than lose the power and capabilities it provides.

Neither possibility absolves him of his earlier actions.

### V-9. The final choices

The player determines how the surviving evidence, political alliances, and neural technology are handled.

Important outcomes include:

- Exposure or suppression of the Ganymede cover-up.
- Accountability for Varga's unauthorized experiments.
- Reform, fragmentation, or continued dominance of WAR.
- Containment, destruction, or limited preservation of Nova.
- Continuation or withdrawal from neural-integration research.
- The state of the player's personal relationships.

These outcomes are related but not identical.

Ari may expose WAR without preserving the romance with Mara.

Ari may remain involved in research while rejecting Nova's coercive integration.

Mara's debt and family circumstances still require realistic resolution.

**End of Act V.**

---

# 9. Epilogue — After the Arena

The default ending should be cautiously hopeful, not perfectly tidy.

Ari's victory and recovered records strengthen the case against WAR's colonial leadership.

Crystal supports public accountability.

Milano attempts to build a reform coalition, with uncertain success.

The technocratic program faces investigation.

Varga's unauthorized experiments are exposed, and Jean-Paul must confront the difference between emotional attachment and excusing misconduct.

Steffan, Dante, and Lyra continue living with the consequences of their decisions. Their relationships are not automatically reconciled.

Ren remains fascinated by the possibilities of human-machine integration but more aware of its risks.

Angel retains important knowledge about Nova's surviving technology.

Mara continues pursuing financial independence and a way to secure her mother's medical care.

Ari and Mara's final relationship depends on trust, respect, and decisions made during the story.

The last image could return to Jupiter, now seen from a quieter location rather than through the enormous public screens of the championship.

The public spectacle has ended. The people who participated in it must live with what happened.

---

# 10. Branching architecture

Rather than creating twelve entirely different campaigns, the game uses a shared event structure with protagonist-specific scenes and selected alternate branches.

## Four principal state dimensions

| State | What it tracks |
|---|---|
| **Trust** | Important interpersonal relationships and alliances |
| **WAR involvement** | Participation in advanced neural research and dependence on institutional support |
| **Evidence** | Discovery, preservation, and disclosure of relevant facts |
| **Integration risk** | Exposure to advanced neural systems and opportunities for Nova to establish access |

Trust should be character-specific rather than one universal relationship score.

There should not be a single morality meter labeling decisions good or evil.

## Major choice points

**C1 — WAR's offer:** Reject, limit, or deepen participation.

**C2 — Romantic conflict:** The player decides how to handle the relationship with Mara or the selected pilot's corresponding personal conflict.

**C3 — Evidence:** Reveal information, share it selectively, or withhold it.

**C4 — Varga:** Expose, investigate further, bargain, or cooperate.

**C5 — Nova:** Choose between containment, destruction, and other technically supported outcomes.

The main tournament remains playable regardless of narrative choices.

## Defeats and alternate routes

A lost championship fight should not necessarily end the cinematic story.

At designated narrative checkpoints, the player may accept the loss and continue through an alternative sequence.

For example, if Ari loses to Mara, Mara advances in the tournament while Ari continues investigating WAR through other encounters.

Arcade mode can retain the conventional retry structure.

This avoids requiring players to lose deliberately for romance while allowing the story to recognize legitimate competitive defeat.

---

# 11. Arcade versus cinematic presentation

| Element | Arcade mode | Cinematic mode |
|---|---|---|
| Tournament introduction | Short cinematic | Full gala and character interactions |
| Qualifiers | Two direct matches | Matches plus contextual scenes |
| Romance | Brief relevant exchanges | Gradual relationship development |
| Investigation | Essential discoveries only | Optional evidence, conversations, character perspectives |
| Midpoint | Kreissack reveal | Investigation and emotional consequences |
| Semifinal | Personalized rivalry | Expanded interpersonal confrontation |
| Championship | Full match | Full match with political and personal setup |
| Nova | Boss transformation and reveal | Additional investigation, intervention, aftermath |
| Ending | Character epilogue | Relationship, political, and technological outcomes |

### Dialogue budget

Most mandatory between-match scenes should last approximately **20–60 seconds**.

Major reveals and emotional confrontations can be longer, but preferably no more than a couple of minutes without a player choice or meaningful transition.

Optional scenes can provide greater depth for players who want to explore the cast.

The goal is not to minimize storytelling at any cost, but to preserve a satisfying alternation between narrative and combat.

---

# 12. Key foreshadowing

| Early clue | Apparent explanation | Eventual significance |
|---|---|---|
| Nova identifiers in current software | Legacy compatibility | Surviving research system |
| Restricted biological supplies | Medical research | Kreissack's preserved brain |
| Abnormal synchronization | Experimental performance improvement | Compatibility with deeper integration |
| Varga's social observations | Performance psychology | Unauthorized emotional experiments |
| Edited romantic scandal footage | Sensationalist broadcasting | Varga's deliberate manipulation |
| Kreissack's unusual commands | Advanced predictive control | Nova overriding biological intentions |
| Technocratic confidential funding | Proprietary research | Collaboration with Kreissack |
| Emergency rescue without biological command | Technocrats overriding Kreissack | Nova's independent decision-making |
| Relay demonstration | Commercial technology showcase | Nova's opportunity to become independent |

Every major twist should have at least two clues before the revelation.

The clues should be visible enough to reward attentive players without making the answer obvious.

---

# 13. Narrative production priorities

The first production slice should contain:

1. Opening ceremony and gala.
2. Ari and Mara's first conversation.
3. Ari versus Selene.
4. A short Steffan–Lyra–Dante scene.
5. A Jean-Paul–Varga exchange.
6. Ari and Mara's first training interaction.
7. A subtle Nova-related technical anomaly.

This slice tests nearly every central component:

- Character charisma.
- Humor and flirtation.
- Fighting-game pacing.
- Public spectacle.
- Relationship development.
- Psychological intrigue.
- Science-fiction mystery.

If the slice works, the remaining chapters can expand the same dramatic rhythm.

---

# 14. Open issues before full screenplay

**O1 — Tournament bracket:** Confirm whether the knockout seeding rules should remain fully fixed or allow limited alternative pairings.

**O2 — Ari and Mara:** Write enough meaningful scenes to make their romance convincing within the tournament's relatively short duration.

**O3 — Mara's debt:** Specify the nature and scale of her financial obligations and realistic solutions that preserve her independence.

**O4 — Varga's research:** Establish how she accesses personal information and manipulates situations without seeming omniscient.

**O5 — Nova's technical constraints:** Finalize the cognitive integration mechanism, local control boundaries, and relay dependency.

**O6 — The rescue:** Establish exactly how Nova obtains access to the facility's safety systems while remaining unable to escape its local infrastructure.

**O7 — Kreissack's reaction:** Define his plausible responses to the discovery that his autonomy was conditional.

**O8 — Branching costs:** Keep alternate routes manageable and ensure every playable pilot has a meaningful ending.

**O9 — HAR design:** Finalize signature models and the two proposed new HARs, Mirage and Helix.

**O10 — Cinematic duration:** Playtest whether the story feels like an exciting fighting game rather than a visual novel interrupted by matches.

---

<!-- SOURCE: 04_Screenplay_Reconstructions/Chapter_01_Anniversary_v0.1.md -->

# ONE MUST FALL 2107
## Chapter 1 — The Anniversary

**Screenplay:** First draft, v0.1  
**Player character:** Ari Solano (default route)  
**Narrative period:** Gala evening through the opening tournament matches  
**Playable fight:** Ari vs. Selene  
**Location:** WAR Anniversary Tournament Complex, near Ganymede

### Scene conventions

- **[CINEMATIC]** — Main animated sequence.
- **[OPTIONAL]** — Expanded cinematic-mode scene.
- **[CHOICE]** — Dialogue selection.
- **[FIGHT]** — Transition to gameplay.
- **[BROADCAST]** — In-universe sporting coverage.
- **[TECHNICAL]** — Production or continuity direction.

Character dialogue is written in English. Ari's pronouns and some visual details adapt to the player's customization.

---

# SCENE 1 — THE LEGEND

**[CINEMATIC — COLD OPEN]**

BLACK SCREEN.

A metallic impact.

Then another.

An old video signal flickers into view.

**ARCHIVAL FOOTAGE — TOURNAMENT, 2097**

The original Nova staggers beneath a series of Jaguar attacks.

The recording has the grain and saturated colors of an old combat broadcast.

A final strike sends Nova collapsing against the arena floor.

The crowd erupts.

**SHIRRO (ARCHIVAL)**

> DOWN! NOVA IS DOWN! CRYSTAL DEVROE HAS DONE THE IMPOSSIBLE!

Rapid cuts.

Crystal raising her arms.

The destruction of a laboratory complex.

News reports announcing Kreissack's presumed death.

Crystal addressing a cheering crowd.

Construction crews working on Ganymede.

A WAR advertisement promising a new age of prosperity.

The images accelerate.

Ten years of development pass in seconds.

New HARs. New cities. New arenas. New corporate leaders.

Finally, the familiar industrial silhouette of Nova disappears behind an enormous anniversary logo.

**SHIRRO (V.O., PRESENT DAY)**

> Ten years ago, a young woman brought down the most feared machine ever built!

The image resolves into a gigantic modern tournament arena suspended against the backdrop of Jupiter.

**SHIRRO**

> Tonight, the legends return—and a new generation comes to knock them off their pedestals!

An array of HAR silhouettes illuminates beneath the words:

**ONE MUST FALL 2107**

**SHIRRO**

> Twelve pilots. One championship. And I, for one, intend to enjoy every minute!

SMASH CUT TO:

---

# SCENE 2 — THE ANNIVERSARY GALA

**[CINEMATIC]**

**INT. ANNIVERSARY COMPLEX — GRAND OBSERVATION HALL — NIGHT**

The gala occupies a vast glass-domed hall overlooking Jupiter.

Golden light glitters across polished metal. Holographic advertisements drift above the guests. Small service robots move through crowds of athletes, celebrities, technicians, journalists, and corporate executives.

Music pulses beneath the conversation.

The atmosphere is extravagant without being entirely formal.

A towering display shows the twelve contestants and their championship odds.

## 2A — The legend and the shadow

Crystal stands beside a display of her original Jaguar.

A guest asks for a photograph.

**GUEST**

> You haven't aged a day!

**CRYSTAL**

> The photograph has. It's ten years old.

The guest laughs without understanding.

After the photograph, Raven approaches.

**RAVEN**

> You still hate these things.

**CRYSTAL**

> Photographs?

**RAVEN**

> Being worshipped.

**CRYSTAL**

> Depends on the worshipper.

A brief smile passes between them.

Raven looks toward the archival display.

**RAVEN**

> They made the old arena look nicer than it was.

**CRYSTAL**

> They left out the blood.

**RAVEN**

> Among other things.

Crystal studies him.

**CRYSTAL**

> Is there something you came to tell me?

**RAVEN**

> I came to say hello.

**CRYSTAL**

> You were never very good at that.

She turns away.

Raven watches her go.

His expression suggests that the conversation is far from finished.

## 2B — Corporate courtship

Across the hall, Milano speaks with Imani Sorell and several executives.

**SORELL**

> The tournament has already exceeded our sponsorship projections.

**MILANO**

> A sporting event is an excellent way to make politicians feel like spectators.

**SORELL**

> And spectators feel like shareholders.

**MILANO**

> I was hoping we might eventually make some of them feel like citizens.

Sorell smiles politely.

**SORELL**

> Try not to reform the company before the opening ceremony, Milano.

Nearby, Selene listens while examining the championship display.

**SELENE**

> Wouldn't that be convenient? Then whoever wins could claim the credit.

Milano turns.

**MILANO**

> Selene Vale. I wondered whether you'd come.

**SELENE**

> I'm competing. It seemed polite to attend.

**MILANO**

> Are you competing to win, or to make someone's life difficult?

**SELENE**

> Ideally both.

She raises her glass.

The exchange is friendly enough to conceal the underlying rivalry.

## 2C — The celebrity triangle

A burst of applause rises near the main entrance.

Lyra Kisaragi enters with Dante Vey.

She wears an extravagant modern outfit that subtly echoes the lines of her Electra HAR.

Fans press toward the barriers.

A child waves a miniature Electra figure.

Lyra notices, stops, and signs it.

**FAN**

> Lyra! Are you going to win?

**LYRA**

> That's the plan. But don't tell the others.

The child laughs.

She turns to face the cameras.

Steffan appears beside her.

**STEFFAN**

> I wouldn't dream of revealing your secrets.

**LYRA**

> Steffan Thomas. And here I was having such a pleasant evening.

**STEFFAN**

> I could improve it.

**DANTE**

> You could improve it by leaving.

**STEFFAN**

> Dante! I didn't see you there.

Steffan looks pointedly at Dante's expensive jacket.

**STEFFAN**

> That's unfortunate. You went to so much trouble.

Lyra laughs before catching Dante's expression.

**LYRA**

> Be nice, both of you.

**STEFFAN**

> I'm being exceptionally nice.

**DANTE**

> You wouldn't recognize nice if it broke your jaw.

**STEFFAN**

> Then perhaps you should introduce us in the arena.

Lyra steps between them—not defensively, but with obvious impatience.

**LYRA**

> Gentlemen, I have fans waiting, a championship to win, and absolutely no intention of spending the evening supervising your egos.

She turns toward the cameras.

Steffan watches her with undisguised interest.

Dante notices.

**DANTE**

> Don't even think about it.

**STEFFAN**

> That's an extraordinary amount of confidence in my imagination.

Dante moves closer.

**DANTE**

> You'll get your fight.

For an instant, Steffan's expression becomes unusually focused.

**STEFFAN**

> I'm counting on it.

The tension lingers a little longer than an ordinary insult would justify.

Then Steffan smiles again and walks away.

## 2D — Two professional liars

**[OPTIONAL CINEMATIC]**

At the quieter end of the hall, Jean-Paul examines a display describing WAR's advanced neural research.

**VARGA (O.S.)**

> Are you interested in the technology, or the information you might obtain from it?

Jean-Paul turns.

Dr. Ilse Varga stands behind him, holding two glasses.

**JEAN-PAUL**

> I was hoping to discover whether the researchers were as interesting as their inventions.

**VARGA**

> A disappointing experiment, I imagine.

**JEAN-PAUL**

> Not so far.

She offers him a glass.

**VARGA**

> Ilse Varga.

**JEAN-PAUL**

> Jean-Paul.

**VARGA**

> I know. I've read your file.

**JEAN-PAUL**

> I hope you enjoyed it.

**VARGA**

> The inconsistencies were particularly entertaining.

Jean-Paul smiles.

**JEAN-PAUL**

> You must have an excellent imagination.

**VARGA**

> I prefer observation. People are more inventive than they realize.

She studies him without embarrassment.

**JEAN-PAUL**

> And what have you observed?

**VARGA**

> You haven't asked me a question you genuinely wanted answered.

He pauses.

**JEAN-PAUL**

> What would you like me to ask?

**VARGA**

> Something you wouldn't want entered into your medical record.

Her smile is faint.

For once, Jean-Paul takes slightly longer than usual to respond.

**JEAN-PAUL**

> Doctor, I suspect we're going to get along terribly.

**VARGA**

> I certainly hope so.

---

# SCENE 3 — THE WRONG KIND OF GUEST

**[CINEMATIC — ARI VIEWPOINT]**

Ari stands near the observation windows, uncomfortable in formal clothing.

Behind Ari, a corporate representative finishes an enthusiastic introduction to two potential sponsors.

**REPRESENTATIVE**

> And with a proper communications strategy, your story could be tremendously inspirational.

**ARI**

> Which part?

**REPRESENTATIVE**

> Overcoming adversity, naturally.

**ARI**

> I'd rather talk about who caused it.

The representative's smile stiffens.

**REPRESENTATIVE**

> Perhaps after the championship.

**ARI**

> That's what everyone keeps saying.

Ari excuses themself.

Near a catering station, Mara Voss has accumulated an impressive number of small plates.

She watches Ari arrive.

**MARA**

> You survived eight minutes. Not bad.

**ARI**

> Were you timing me?

**MARA**

> No. They were.

Mara points discreetly toward the sponsors.

**MARA**

> You ruined their pitch.

**ARI**

> I answered their question.

**MARA**

> Exactly. That's why they're upset.

Ari looks at her plates.

**ARI**

> Planning to feed a small army?

**MARA**

> Have you seen what they charge for admission?

**ARI**

> You're a contestant. You didn't pay.

**MARA**

> Neither did the food. Doesn't mean I shouldn't liberate it.

She offers Ari a pastry.

**ARI**

> Does it taste as expensive as it looks?

**MARA**

> No. That's the impressive part.

Ari takes it.

For a moment, both look out at Jupiter.

**MARA**

> So. You're the Ganymede troublemaker.

**ARI**

> Depends who's telling the story.

**MARA**

> WAR says you're difficult.

**ARI**

> WAR says a lot of things.

**MARA**

> That's true. They called me a promising independent competitor.

**ARI**

> Are you?

**MARA**

> I'm independent. And I've made quite a few promises.

She smiles.

Ari smiles back despite themself.

### [CHOICE 1 — ARI'S RESPONSE]

**A. Playful**

**ARI:** You're not taking this seriously at all, are you?

**MARA:** Oh, I'm taking the fighting seriously. The rest of it is an expensive costume party.

**ARI:** You have a costume.

**MARA:** I borrowed a jacket. Very different.

**B. Curious**

**ARI:** What brought you here?

**MARA:** Besides the world's most aggressively decorated buffet?

**ARI:** Besides that.

**MARA:** Money. A good fight. Maybe a little luck. You?

**ARI:** Something I need to settle.

**MARA:** That sounds considerably less fun.

**C. Flirtatious**

**ARI:** You seem to be enjoying yourself.

**MARA:** I was. Then this attractive stranger interrupted my dinner.

**ARI:** Should I leave?

**MARA:** That depends. Can you reach those pastries?

**[All responses converge.]**

A loud burst of applause rises from the opposite side of the hall.

Steffan has begun posing for photographs with a group of fans.

**MARA**

> That's Thomas. He's been insufferable since he learned to walk.

**ARI**

> You know him?

**MARA**

> Everybody knows him. Most of us didn't ask to.

**ARI**

> He's a good fighter.

**MARA**

> Unfortunately. It's his most annoying quality.

Ari laughs.

Mara studies Ari more carefully.

**MARA**

> You'll do better here if you occasionally remember to enjoy yourself.

**ARI**

> Is that professional advice?

**MARA**

> No. Professional advice costs extra.

A pause.

**MARA**

> Mara, by the way.

**ARI**

> Ari.

**MARA**

> I know.

She takes one last pastry.

**MARA**

> You were on the giant screen when you arrived.

She starts to leave.

**ARI**

> See you in the arena?

Mara looks back.

**MARA**

> Unless you're afraid I'll win.

She disappears into the crowd.

Ari watches her leave, smiling.

---

# SCENE 4 — A VERY OLD GHOST

**[SHORT CINEMATIC — MYSTERY HOOK]**

**INT. TECHNICAL EXHIBITION ROOM — NIGHT**

A crowd watches a demonstration of WAR's experimental neural-control equipment.

Ren Akiyama studies the performance readouts with fascination.

Angel stands at an adjacent diagnostic terminal.

**REN**

> The prediction latency is lower than the published figure.

**ANGEL**

> They haven't published the real figure.

**REN**

> How can you be sure?

**ANGEL**

> Because that's what the real figure looks like.

Ren leans closer.

**REN**

> You think they have a new architecture?

**ANGEL**

> I think somebody's forgotten to clean up after an old one.

A line flashes across the terminal:
